       1 +SET NOCOUNT ON;                                                
       2 +DECLARE @account int = $(account), @tz sysname = '$(tz)', @week
         + date = '$(week)', @type varchar(40) = '$(type)';              
       3 +WITH raw AS (                                                  
       4 +  SELECT location, event_type, occurred_at, duration_seconds, o
         +utcome,                                                        
       5 +         CAST(CAST(occurred_at AS datetimeoffset) AT TIME ZONE 
         +@tz AS date) AS d                                              
       6 +  FROM activity_events WHERE account_id = @account             
       7 +), w AS (                                                      
       8 +  SELECT *, DATEADD(day, -(DATEDIFF(day,'19000101',d) % 7), d) 
         +AS ws FROM raw                                                 
       9 +), dd AS (                                                     
      10 +  SELECT location, event_type, ws FROM w GROUP BY location, eve
         +nt_type, occurred_at, duration_seconds, outcome, ws            
      11 +)                                                              
      12 +SELECT location, ws, COUNT(*) n FROM dd                        
      13 +WHERE (@type = 'all' OR event_type = @type) AND ws >= DATEADD(d
         +ay,-56,@week) AND ws <= @week                                  
      14 +  AND location IN ($(locs))                                    
      15 +GROUP BY location, ws ORDER BY location, ws;                   

⏺ Confirmo con SQL manual que la API funciona correctamente: con Citas =
  0,6,0,0,0,0,2,1, la mediana y MAD dan 0, el piso queda en 0, y por eso el

  en series dispersas, así que lo dejo documentado como hallazgo sin modificar
  el código.

      123  2. **§2 and §5, the back-test.** The flag rates (6 %, 36 %, 51
            %, 55 %) and the locations flagged for the week of Jul 20 cam
           e from an exploratory run that bucketed weeks in UTC. The app 
           buckets weeks in each account's local time, so exact counts ca
           n differ slightly. Measured by the app: the chosen rule flags 
           **73 of the same 1,173 location-weeks (6.2 %)**, and 92 of 1,3
           96 (6.6 %) if every location-week with at least 4 weeks of his
           tory is included. For the week of 2026-07-20 it flags the same
            three locations, all `above`: account 12 Site F (11, normal 1
           –10), account 15 Site C (12, normal 2–10), account 19 Site A (
           13, normal 2–12). In local weeks, account 6's spike week holds
            880 events (not 881). Its baseline for Jul 20 is `53, 880, 10
           2, 59, 76, 69, 79, 50`, which gives a median of 72.5 and a nor
           mal range of 24–121, instead of §7's UTC series with a median 
           of 72. The unit test keeps §7's series as a pure input; the in
           tegration tests use the local numbers.
      124  3. **§10, time budget.** Real figures: planning about 1 h (12:
           20 to 13:20); walking skeleton and docs about 15 min, with two
            terminals in parallel. The evening block stays at 4 h and Sun
           day at 0.5 h. Actual time per block is reported above.
      125  
      126 +## Known limitations                                          
      127 +                                                              
      128 +- **The history rule is conservative.** A baseline week counts
          + only if it starts at or after the location's first event, so 
          +a location whose first event lands on a Monday after midnight 
          +loses that whole week. In the seed, account 4 (America/Chicago
          +) on the week of Mar 2: Site D (first event Sun Feb 1) has its
          + 4 weeks and a range, while Sites A, B and C (first events Mon
          + Feb 2 at 06:17 and 11:11, and Tue Feb 3 at 02:01, local) have
          + 3 and show "No baseline yet", although the account as a whole
          + has a range. The cost is one extra week of waiting for a new 
          +location; the alternative would let a partial first week drag 
          +its median down.                                              
      129 +                                                              
      130  ## Deferred, and why
      131  
      132  - **Quality metrics** (missed-call rate, no-show rate): a diff
           erent question from volume, with its own baseline problems (ra
           tes on tiny denominators).

    solo los mensajes del navegador cuando la API responde 400 o 500.

    celdas para las cuentas 12, 4 (la semana del cambio de hora), 7 (Phoenix) y
    18 (UTC). Site F de la cuenta 12 también cuadra con el cálculo a mano
    (mediana 5,5, rango 1–10).
  - Recorrí toda la API (2.000 peticiones): la regla marca 92 de 1.396

    existe en el seed y se ve en ámbar con su etiqueta.
  - El reporte está en ai-log/e2e-report.md (249f4dd).

  Hallazgos para la sesión de planificación
  - F1 (major, vista y contrato): cuando una semana tiene menos de 8 semanas de
    historia (del 2 al 23 de marzo), la vista dice "Compared with your own 8
    previous full weeks (Jan 5 – Mar 1)" y el gráfico dibuja como ceros las
    semanas anteriores a los datos. En realidad el rango sale de 4 semanas. Esto
    choca con el plan, porque el contrato fija baseline.weeks en 8.
  - F2 (major, regla de "normal"): si la mediana es 0, el rango queda en 0–0. En
    la cuenta 6, filtro de citas, semana del 20 de julio, 4 de 15 sedes salen
    above; Site M, por ejemplo, tuvo 2 y 1 citas en las dos semanas anteriores.
    El SQL confirma que la API aplica bien la regla. En todo el seed hay 199
    filas así. Decidirlo le toca a planificación.
  - F3–F6 (minor, vista):
    - Con una semana que no está en availableWeeks, el selector muestra otra
      semana distinta de la de los datos.
    - Con una semana anterior a los datos, la vista dice "No activity recorded
      yet" de una cuenta que sí tiene actividad.
    - Con account=999, type=foo o week=xyz, la vista vuelve a los valores por
      defecto sin avisar y la URL conserva el valor inválido.
    - El estado de error no tiene botón "Try again": para la misma cuenta solo
      se sale recargando.
  - F9 y F10: solo notas, sin corrección propuesta.

      64  
      65  ## What "normal" means
      66  
      67 -For each series (a location, or the account as a whole): median
         - ± 2σ with σ = max(1.4826·MAD, √median), shown as whole numbers
         - ("normal 24–121"). A value equal to a limit is typical. Fewer 
         -than 4 weeks of history → `insufficient_history`.              
      67 +For each series (a location, or the account as a whole): median
         + ± 2σ with σ = max(1.4826·MAD, √median, 1), shown as whole numb
         +ers ("normal 24–121"). A value equal to a limit is typical. Few
         +er than 4 weeks of history → `insufficient_history`.           
      68  
      69  - **Median and MAD** ignore how big an outlier is. Account 6's 
          880-event spike week sits in the baseline of the following eigh
          t weeks and does not move their range; it is listed as an unusu
          al baseline week instead of being hidden.
      70  - **The √median floor** is the noise you expect from small coun
          ts (2 to 15 events per location-week). Without it, a location w
          ith the same count every week gets a zero-width range.
      71  - **Why not percentages, quartiles or a moving average**: measu
          red against the seed, this rule flags about 6 % of location-wee
          ks; the alternatives flag 36 % to 55 % (PLAN.md §5). A screen t
          hat flags half the locations every Monday gets ignored.
      72  - **History** starts at the location's first event, and only co
          mplete weeks count: a baseline week counts if it starts at or a
          fter the first event. The partial week of the first event is le
          ft out so a half week does not drag the median down. The same r
          ule decides which weeks can be picked: `availableWeeks` starts 
          at the first complete week (Feb 2 for every account in the seed
          , whose first events are on Sun Feb 1). The response carries `h
          istoryWeeks`, the exact number that decided `insufficient_histo
          ry`.
      73 -- **All-zero baseline** (for example, a location that has never
         - had an appointment): by the formula the range is 0–0, and a si
         -ngle event is `above`. That is pinned in a test as a visible ch
         -oice.                                                          
      73 +- **A floor of one event for σ.** When the median is 0 (a locat
         +ion that rarely or never has appointments), √median is 0 as wel
         +l, and before the floor the range collapsed to 0–0: a single ev
         +ent was `above`. With σ ≥ 1 the range is never narrower than ±2
         + events, so that location reads "normal 0–2". It only changes s
         +eries whose median is below 1; see Deviations, item 11.        
      74  
      75  ## Decisions and trade-offs
      76  
     ...
      115  9. **Integration tests call the service directly** with the se
           eded database (as planned: no `WebApplicationFactory`). The HT
           TP mapping (404, 400, 422 as `ProblemDetails`) was checked wit
           h curl, and the query-string validation is a pure function wit
           h its own tests. A non-numeric account id (`/api/accounts/abc/
           ...`) gets a 404 from the route constraint, not a 400.
      116  10. **The partial week of the first event is neither history n
           or pickable.** The plan said "fewer than 4 complete weeks sinc
           e its first event" and "`availableWeeks` from the first week w
           ith data". In the seed, the first week with data (Jan 26) hold
           s only its Sunday. A baseline week counts as history only if i
           t starts at or after the first event, and `availableWeeks` fol
           lows the same rule, so it starts at Feb 2. A `week=2026-01-26`
            passed by hand is still accepted and comes out `insufficient_
           history`.
      117  
      118 +11. **σ is floored at 1: σ = max(1.4826·MAD, √median, 1).** A 
          +degenerate case of PLAN.md §5's rule, found by the end-to-end 
          +pass (`ai-log/e2e-report.md`, F2). With a median of 0, MAD and
          + √median are both 0 and the range was 0–0. Account 6, appointm
          +ents, week of Jul 20: Site M (0, 6, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 1 in its ba
          +seline) was `above` with "normal 0–0", and so were three other
          + locations with 2 appointments. Across the seed, 199 location-
          +weeks were `above` against a 0–0 range. Measured over every lo
          +cation-week with a range (1,396 per type), before → after: all
          + 92 → 92 (6.6 %), calls 78 → 78 (5.6 %), leads 149 → 95 (10.7 
          +% → 6.8 %), appointments 252 → 74 (18.1 % → 5.3 %), all four t
          +ypes together 571 → 339 (10.2 % → 6.1 %); 0–0 rows flagged 199
          + → 0. Series with a median of 1 or more are unchanged, so the 
          +errata's 6.2 % and 6.6 % for all activity stand. The unit test
          + that pinned "all-zero baseline: one event is above" was chang
          +ed on purpose to the new range (0–2).

      115  9. **Integration tests call the service directly** with the se
           eded database (as planned: no `WebApplicationFactory`). The HT
           TP mapping (404, 400, 422 as `ProblemDetails`) was checked wit
           h curl, and the query-string validation is a pure function wit
           h its own tests. A non-numeric account id (`/api/accounts/abc/
           ...`) gets a 404 from the route constraint, not a 400.
      116  10. **The partial week of the first event is neither history n
           or pickable.** The plan said "fewer than 4 complete weeks sinc
           e its first event" and "`availableWeeks` from the first week w
           ith data". In the seed, the first week with data (Jan 26) hold
           s only its Sunday. A baseline week counts as history only if i
           t starts at or after the first event, and `availableWeeks` fol
           lows the same rule, so it starts at Feb 2. A `week=2026-01-26`
            passed by hand is still accepted and comes out `insufficient_
           history`.
      117  11. **σ is floored at 1: σ = max(1.4826·MAD, √median, 1).** A 
           degenerate case of PLAN.md §5's rule, found by the end-to-end 
           pass (`ai-log/e2e-report.md`, F2). With a median of 0, MAD and
            √median are both 0 and the range was 0–0. Account 6, appointm
           ents, week of Jul 20: Site M (0, 6, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 1 in its ba
           seline) was `above` with "normal 0–0", and so were three other
            locations with 2 appointments. Across the seed, 199 location-
           weeks were `above` against a 0–0 range. Measured over every lo
           cation-week with a range (1,396 per type), before → after: all
            92 → 92 (6.6 %), calls 78 → 78 (5.6 %), leads 149 → 95 (10.7 
           % → 6.8 %), appointments 252 → 74 (18.1 % → 5.3 %), all four t
           ypes together 571 → 339 (10.2 % → 6.1 %); 0–0 rows flagged 199
            → 0. Series with a median of 1 or more are unchanged, so the 
           errata's 6.2 % and 6.6 % for all activity stand. The unit test
            that pinned "all-zero baseline: one event is above" was chang
           ed on purpose to the new range (0–2).
      118 +12. **`inBaseline` on every `history[]` item** (in `metrics[]`
          + and `locations[]`): a contract change approved after the end-
          +to-end pass (F1). With fewer than 8 weeks of history (account 
          +6, week of Mar 2) the view said "Compared with your own 8 prev
          +ious full weeks (Jan 5 – Mar 1)" and drew the weeks before any
          + data as zeros, while the range came from 4 weeks. The flag is
          + set from the same list of weeks that gives `historyWeeks`, so
          + the two cannot disagree. The view now reads the count and the
          + first week from the API ("4 previous full weeks (Feb 2 – Mar 
          +1)") and draws the weeks outside the baseline dimmed and label
          +ed "not used". `baseline {start, end, weeks}` keeps describing
          + the 8-week window shown.

⏺ Encontré que toObservable solo emite recién en el primer ciclo de detección,
  lo que rompía los tests al pedirse la lista de cuentas en la construcción del
  componente. Voy a usar un BehaviorSubject para las cuentas, así se conserva el
  momento original de la petición.

  commit de F5:

⏺ Bash(cd ~/projects/site-pulse && python3 - <<'PY'
      p='README.md'; s=open(p).read()…)
  ⎿  1631ce4 fix(web): rewrite invalid URL filters to the values shown with
      replaceUrl
  ⎿  Updated README.md (+1 -1)
      79  - **Week boundaries in C#, counting in SQL.** The accounts stor
          e IANA zone names, which T-SQL does not understand. C# computes
           the 10 boundaries with `TimeZoneInfo` (DST weeks are 167 or 16
          9 hours) and passes them as UTC. The predicate stays a plain ra
          nge on `occurred_at`, which can seek the `(account_id, occurred
          _at)` index. Converting every row with `AT TIME ZONE` inside th
          e query would not.
      80  - **Duplicates handled at read time.** The 12 double-ingested r
          ows stay in the table as delivered. The view `activity_events_d
          eduped` (`SELECT DISTINCT` over every column but `id`) is the o
          ne place the rule lives. The response reports how many were dro
          pped from the weeks shown.
      81  - **Pinned clock.** The seed ends on Mon 2026-07-27; with the r
          eal clock every week would be empty. `TimeProvider` is injected
          , and Development pins it to 2026-07-27 14:00 UTC. The UI says 
          so.
      82 -- **State in the URL.** `account`, `type` and `week` query para
         -ms are the only source of truth for filters: reload-safe, share
         -able, no state library.                                        
      82 +- **State in the URL.** `account`, `type` and `week` query para
         +ms are the only source of truth for filters: reload-safe, share
         +able, no state library. An invalid value (`account=999`, `type=
         +foo`, `week=xyz`) falls back to the default and the URL is rewr
         +itten in place (`replaceUrl`) to what is actually shown, so the
         + address bar never names an account or filter the page is not s
         +howing. There is no on-screen notice for it; that was a deliber
         +ate scope call after the end-to-end pass (F5).                 
      83  - **No cache.** Measured: 20 requests for the largest account (
          15 locations) take a median of 19 ms end to end, with a max of 
          35 ms. At scale, closed weeks never change, so the move would b
          e caching per (account, week, type) or a weekly summary materia
          lized per account-local week. Today that would be complexity wi
          th no measurable gain.
      84

      61  - **For us** = each location against **its own** previous 8 com
          plete weeks, not against other locations or accounts.
      62  - **Act on it** = rank by how far outside the range a location 
          is, and always show what it was compared against.
      63  - **Metric** = weekly event volume, filterable by type. Quality
           (missed-call rate, no-shows) is a different question and is de
          ferred.
      64 +- **The screen explains its own gaps.** When data is missing, t
         +hin or odd (no events yet, too little history, a zero week, a s
         +pike, duplicates, the week still in progress), nothing is chang
         +ed or hidden: the page says what it is not showing and why, fro
         +m what the API sends, so a partial picture is not mistaken for 
         +a broken system.                                               
      65  
      66  ## What "normal" means
      67  
     ...
       99  
      100  Also covered: account 20 (no events) → `no_data` on all four m
           etrics and no locations; account 999 → not found; per-type loc
           ation counts add up to the total; an early week (account 6, Fe
           b 16) reports `historyWeeks` that were computed by hand.
      101  
      102 +**End-to-end pass.** On the evening of 2026-10-03 the whole ap
          +p (dev server, API, seeded database) was tested in a real brow
          +ser, 18 cases, each screen diffed field by field against the A
          +PI response for the same URL, and the API checked again agains
          +t `verify-aggregates.sql` (accounts 12, 4 in its DST week, 7 a
          +nd 18). The first pass found 10 issues; the planning session d
          +ecided which to fix; each fix went in with a test that failed 
          +first, and a second pass of the full matrix came out clean, wi
          +th one data condition left for later (exceptional weeks per lo
          +cation). Cases, findings, classification, before/after screens
          +hots: [`ai-log/e2e-report.md`](ai-log/e2e-report.md).         
      103 +                                                              
      104 +![Default view](ai-log/e2e/default-view.png)                  
      105 +                                                              
      106  ## Security, sized to the slice
      107  
      108  Authentication is out of scope per the prompt, so the account 
           id comes from the route. **In production it would come from th
           e session token, never from a client-supplied value.** Within 
           that: the API is GET-only; every query has `account_id` in its
            predicate; all SQL is parameterized; `eventType` is a whiteli
           st; errors are `ProblemDetails` without stack traces; no open 
           CORS (the Angular dev server proxies `/api`); no `innerHTML` i
           n the view; and the SA password is a labeled local-dev default
            that can be overridden.
     ...

           eeded database) so status codes and JSON shape are pinned in C
           I, not just checked with curl.

          -` in the location detail, not only at account level.          
      148 +- Keep the layout still while a week loads (the week picker an
          +d clock disappear and come back), and keep a bar's value label
          + clear of the median line in the detail chart (end-to-end note
          +s F10).                                                       
      149 +- `unusualBaselineWeeks` per location, so the location detail 
          +explains a spike in its own chart as the account view does (en
          +d-to-end finding F11; a contract change).                     
      150  - Quality metrics as a second tab, starting with missed-call r
           ate, with a minimum-volume rule before any status is shown.
      151  - Run the integration tests in CI against the compose database
           , and add a seeded-database health check to `docker compose`.

⏺ Terminé los 8 puntos de la validación antes del corte de las 23:15, así que no
  hubo que recortar nada. En el plan quedan tildadas las tareas 1–6 y la
  entrega está escrita en la sección de Q4. Tests finales: dotnet test 59/59, ng
  test 40/40 y ng build sin advertencias.

  Correcciones (una por commit, cada test visto fallar primero)
  - F2, la regla (036d757): ahora σ = max(1,4826·MAD, √mediana, 1). Lo medí
    antes y después sobre 1.396 sedes-semana por tipo:

  | Tipo                      | Antes        | Después     |
  |---------------------------|--------------|-------------|
  | Leads                     | 149          | 95          |
  | Appointments              | 252          | 74          |
  | Total (4 tipos)           | 571 (10,2 %) | 339 (6,1 %) |
  | Filas above con rango 0–0 | 199          | 0           |
    All y Calls no cambian, así que la errata del README (6,2 % y 6,6 %) sigue
    valiendo. El test de "base toda en cero" cambió a propósito; lo dicen el
    mensaje del commit y la desviación 11 del README. Agregué la línea fija en
    "How we decide what is normal".
  - F1, inBaseline (58fa6ba): se calcula con las mismas semanas que
    historyWeeks, y un test comprueba que ambos coinciden. La vista ahora dice
    "Compared with your own 4 previous full weeks (Feb 2 – Mar 1)" y dibuja las
    semanas fuera de la base atenuadas con el rótulo "not used". Es la
    desviación 12.
  - F3 y F4 (0f6b9f3): una semana anterior a la primera semana completa responde
    400 y nombra esa semana (2026-02-02). La cuenta sin eventos sigue en 200
    no_data.
  - F6 (051e0e6): botón "Try again" en los dos errores. Aparece solo cuando no
    hubo respuesta o fue un 5xx; ante un 400 no tiene sentido repetir.
  - F5 (1631ce4): la URL inválida se reescribe con replaceUrl a lo que se
    muestra, sin aviso en pantalla, y está nombrado en el README.

  500 real: con el contenedor de la base detenido, la API en Development
  devuelve ProblemDetails sin traza. No hubo nada que corregir y tildé "no stack
  traces" en el CHECKLIST.

  Segunda pasada M1–M18: en 22 URLs la vista coincide con la API sin
  diferencias, y M17 vuelve a cuadrar con el SQL a mano. En M18 cada condición
  de datos tiene un texto visible que la explica; tres de esas explicaciones no
  tenían test y ahora sí. El reporte tiene la columna de clasificación y las
  capturas de antes y después, y el README la línea "the screen explains its own
  gaps".

      121  10. **The partial week of the first event is neither history n
           or pickable.** The plan said "fewer than 4 complete weeks sinc
           e its first event" and "`availableWeeks` from the first week w
           ith data". In the seed, the first week with data (Jan 26) hold
           s only its Sunday. A baseline week counts as history only if i
           t starts at or after the first event, and `availableWeeks` fol
           lows the same rule, so it starts at Feb 2. A `week` before tha
           t first complete week, passed by hand, is a 400 that names the
            first complete week (end-to-end findings F3 and F4: the view 
           showed the week picker on another week, or "No activity record
           ed yet" for an account with data). An account with no events a
           t all still answers 200 with `no_data` for any finished week.
      122  11. **σ is floored at 1: σ = max(1.4826·MAD, √median, 1).** A 
           degenerate case of PLAN.md §5's rule, found by the end-to-end 
           pass (`ai-log/e2e-report.md`, F2). With a median of 0, MAD and
            √median are both 0 and the range was 0–0. Account 6, appointm
           ents, week of Jul 20: Site M (0, 6, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 1 in its ba
           seline) was `above` with "normal 0–0", and so were three other
            locations with 2 appointments. Across the seed, 199 location-
           weeks were `above` against a 0–0 range. Measured over every lo
           cation-week with a range (1,396 per type), before → after: all
            92 → 92 (6.6 %), calls 78 → 78 (5.6 %), leads 149 → 95 (10.7 
           % → 6.8 %), appointments 252 → 74 (18.1 % → 5.3 %), all four t
           ypes together 571 → 339 (10.2 % → 6.1 %); 0–0 rows flagged 199
            → 0. Series with a median of 1 or more are unchanged, so the 
           errata's 6.2 % and 6.6 % for all activity stand. The unit test
            that pinned "all-zero baseline: one event is above" was chang
           ed on purpose to the new range (0–2).
      123  12. **`inBaseline` on every `history[]` item** (in `metrics[]`

           ious full weeks (Jan 5 – Mar 1)" and drew the weeks before any
            data as zeros, while the range came from 4 weeks. The flag is
            set from the same list of weeks that gives `historyWeeks`, so
            the two cannot disagree. The view now reads the count and the

           1)") and draws the weeks outside the baseline dimmed and label
           ed "not used". `baseline {start, end, weeks}` keeps describing
            the 8-week window shown.
